     B868 20F4     
4219               	;
4220               	; 	' HARRY STARTS ON FLOOR 2 AT THE WEST EDGE OF SCREEN 7, and that is as
4221               	; 	' far left as the clock allows. The research says "the second-floor
4222               	; 	' elevator door", which is screen 3, and it cannot be done:
4223               	; 	'
4224               	; 	'   spawn      escape     round
4225               	; 	'   screen 3   119.9 s    100 s   -- impossible
4226               	; 	'   screen 5   108.1 s    100 s   -- impossible
4227               	; 	'   screen 6   102.3 s    100 s   -- impossible
4228               	; 	'   screen 7    96.4 s    100 s   -- 3.6 s of room
4229               	; 	'
4230               	; 	' HE STARTS AT THE LIFT NOW, AND THAT COST HIM A SPEED CHANGE.
4231               	; 	'
4232               	; 	' His escape route ZIGZAGS -- east along floor 2 to its escalator, WEST
4233               	; 	' along floor 3 to that one, then east again along the roof to the door.
4234               	; 	' From the lift on screen 3 that is 5,120 px against the 4,208 he walked
4235               	; 	' from screen 7, so at his old 1.75 px a pass he needed 105 s of a 100 s
4236               	; 	' round: he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4237               	; 	' would quietly have stopped existing. This block used to say exactly that
4238               	; 	' and conclude the lift was impossible. It was impossible AT THAT SPEED.
4239               	; 	'
4240               	; 	' AND QUARTER-PIXELS COULD NOT EXPRESS THE ANSWER. A quarter of a pixel
4241               	; 	' per pass is worth about TEN SECONDS of his route, so the two candidate
4242               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4243               	; 	'
4244               	; 	'   2.00 px/pass   escapes at 102.4 s of a 100 s round   never escapes
4245               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early
4246               	; 	'
4247               	; 	' Worse, 2.25 was not reachable at all: the accumulator below had two
4248               	; 	' drain steps and therefore a hard ceiling of 2 px a pass, so hsp4 = 9
4249               	; 	' silently delivered 2.00 and he fell two thirds of a screen short of his
4250               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4251               	; 	'
4252               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,
4253               	; 	' and hsp64 = 35 (2.1875 px/pass) lands him at 96.3 s of the 100 --
4254               	; 	' escaping as the timer expires, which is what was asked for.
4255               	; 	'
4256               	; 	' 35 AND NOT 34, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE PLAYER'S OWN SCREEN. Measured
4257               	; 	' in play at 34: standing on an ordinary screen he escaped with one timer
4258               	; 	' unit left, and standing on the ELEVATOR screen he ran out of clock --
4259               	; 	' same route, same constant, decided by where the player happened to be.
4260               	; 	' Everything that moves is paced per loop PASS while the clock is paced in
4261               	; 	' real FRAMES, so a busy screen slows Harry in real time and the clock does
4262               	; 	' not slow with him. A whole round measured 2,335 passes over about 98 s --
4263               	; 	' 23.8 a second, not the 25 the model assumes -- and the heavy screens are
4264               	; 	' slower again. 35 buys the margin that covers the worst of them. The
4265               	; 	' underlying split is recorded in DESIGN.md 0f-ter; this constant only
4266               	; 	' papers over it.
4267               	; 	'
4268               	; 	' The margin
4269               	; 	' that remains is the ROUND's, not the chase's: Kelly still has to get
4270               	; 	' there first, and does. assets/checkchase.py walks both routes out of
4271               	; 	' these constants, models the drain ceiling, and fails the build if the
4272               	; 	' configured speed is one the accumulator cannot deliver.
4273               	; 	hlv = 1
4274 B86A 0200  20 	li r0,256   ; 1*256
     B86C 0100     
4275 B86E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_HLV
     B870 218B

4436               	;
4437               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4438               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4439               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4440               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4441               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4442               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4443               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4444               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4445               	; 	'
4446               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4447               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4448               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4449               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4450               	; 	'
4451               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4452               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4453               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4454               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4455               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4456               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4457               	; 	' anybody takes.
4458               	; 	'
4459               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4460               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4461               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4462               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.
4463               	; 	hsp64 = 57			' 0.891 px per FRAME, every Krook
4464 B9C0 0200  20 	li r0,14592   ; 57*256
     B9C2 3900

4488 B9F4 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4489 B9F6 0450  20 	b *r0
4490               	;
4491               	; 	' ======================================================================
4492               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4493               	; 	' ======================================================================
4494               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4495               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4496               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4497               	; 	' of running.
4498               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4499               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4500               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4501               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4502               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4503               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4504               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4505               	; 	'
4506               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4507               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4508               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4509               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4510               	; 	'
4511               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4512               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4513               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4514               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4515               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4516               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4517               	; 	'
4518               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4519               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4520               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4521               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4522               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4523               	; 	'
4524               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4525               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4526               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4527               	; draw_screen:
4528               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4529               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4530 B9F8 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B9FA A980     
4531 B9FC EAFE     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4532               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4533 B9FE 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     BA00 A980

4535               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4536 BA04 04C0  14 	clr r0
4537 BA06 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     BA08 219E     
4538               cv151
4539               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4540               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4541               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4542               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4543               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4544               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4545               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4546               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4547               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4548               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.

4638               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4639               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4640               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4641               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4642               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4643               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4644               	; 	'
4645               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4646               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4647               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4648               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4649               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4650               	; 	'
4651               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4652               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4653               	; 	' above it at all.
4654               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4655               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4656               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4657               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4658               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4659               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4660               	; 	'
4661               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4662               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.

5198               	; 			' AND HOW MANY FLOORS CARRY ONE AT ALL. The gates above

5205               	; 			' Krook 1 was a ball on all FOUR floors of every populated

5258               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
5259               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
5260               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
5261               	; 			'
5262               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
5263               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
5264               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
5265               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
5266               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
5267               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
5268               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
5269               	; 			'
5270               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
5271               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
5272               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
5273               	; 			'
5274               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
5275               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs

5353               cv230
5354               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5355               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5356               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5357               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5358               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5359               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5360               	; 			'
5361               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5362               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5363               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5364               	; 			'
5365               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5366               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5367               	; 			'
5368               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5369               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5370               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5371               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5372               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5373               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5374               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5375               	; 			' reported from play.
5376               	; 			'
5377               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5378               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5379               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5380               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5381               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5382               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5383               	; 			'
5384               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5385               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5386               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5387               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5388               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5389               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5390               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5391               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5392               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5393               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5394               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5395               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5396               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5397               	; 			stag = lx AND 63

5477 C0E2 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5478               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5479               	; 			'
5480               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5481               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5482               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5483               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5484               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5485               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5486               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5487               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5488               	; 			'
5489               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5490               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5491               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5492               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5493               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5494               	; 			'
5495               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5496               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5497               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5498               	; 			'
5499               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5500               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5501               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5502               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5503               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5504               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5505               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5506 C0E4 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     C0E6 21D5

6161               	; 	' ONE CELL OF THE DOORWAY, from its column (ccl 0-3), its band row

6164               	; 	' edges: column 0 and column 3 carry the JAMB, row 0 carries the LINTEL
6165               	; 	' and row 3 the SILL.
6166               	; 	'
6167               	; 	' THE SILL IS REVEALED AS THE DOORS PART, which falls out of this rather
6168               	; 	' than being arranged: shut, every cell is plain door and there is no
6169               	; 	' sill to see; part-open, only the middle two columns show car and sill;
6170               	; 	' open, all four do. A sill sitting under a shut door would be a ledge

6399               cv322
6400               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6401               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6402               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6403               	; 		'
6404               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6405               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6406               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6407               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6408               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6409               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6410               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6411               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6412               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6413               	; 		'
6414               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6415               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6416               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6417               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6418               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6419               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
6420 C82E D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6721               	;
6722               	; 	' --- running
6723               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6724               	; 	'
6725               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6726               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6727               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6728               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6729               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6730               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6731               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6732               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6733               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6734               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.

7050               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
7051               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7052               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7053               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7054               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7055               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7056               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7057               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7058               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7059               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7060               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7061               	; 	'
7062               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7063               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7064               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7065               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at

7625               	;
7626               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7627               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7628               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7629               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7630               	; 	'
7631               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7632               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7633               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7634               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7635               	; 	'
7636               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7637               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7638               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7639               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7640               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7641               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7642               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7643               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7644               	; 	'
7645               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7646               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7647               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7648               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7649               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7650               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7651               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7652               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7653               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7654               	; 	'
7655               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7656               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7657               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7658               	; wipe_shelf:

7734               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7735               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7736               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7737               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7738               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7739               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7740               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7741               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7742               	; 	' nothing.
7743               	; 	'
7744               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7745               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7746               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7747               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no

7807               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the

7811               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round

8732               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
8733               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
8734               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
8735               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
8736               	; 	' runs every pass.
8737               	; 	'
8738               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
8739               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
8740               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
8741               	; 	'
8742               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
8743               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
8744               	; 	'
8745               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
8746               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
8747               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
8748               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
8749               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
8750               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
8751               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
8752               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
8753               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
8754               	; 	'
8755               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8756               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8757               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8758               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8759               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8760               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8761               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8762               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8763               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8764               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8765               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8766               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8767               	; 	'
8768               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8769               	; 	'
8770               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8771               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8772               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8773               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8774               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8775               	; 	'
8776               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
8777               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
8778               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
8779               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
8780               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN

8861               cv597
8862               	;
8863               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
8864               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
8865               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
8866               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
8867               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
8868               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
8869               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
8870               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
8871               	; 	'
8872               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
8873               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
8874               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
8875               	; 	'
8876               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
8877               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
8878               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
8879               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
8880               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
8881               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
8882               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
8883               	; 	'
8884               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
8885               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
8886               	; 	'
8887               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
8888               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
8889               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
8890               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
8891               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
8892               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
8893               	; 	'
8894               	; 	' FIVE FRAMES, AND THREE WAS TOO FEW. With two drains he moves at most 2 px
8895               	; 	' a frame, so five frames is 10 px -- and to skip the window he would have
8896               	; 	' to start outside it on one side and land outside it on the other, which
8897               	; 	' needs a step of 12. Ten is safe with a pixel to spare, and 10 + 4 = 14
8898               	; 	' against a 23-wide catch band.
8899               	; 	'
8900               	; 	' It was three, and three was the same bug in miniature. The lift screen
8901               	; 	' runs at almost exactly 20 passes a second, which IS fdv = 3, so every
8902               	; 	' momentary hitch there pushed past the clamp and he lost that frame --
8903               	; 	' measured as TIME 02 left on the lift screen against TIME 04 on a light
8904               	; 	' one, where before the real-time change it had been 00 against 09. The
8905               	; 	' clamp only exists for a genuine stall, and at five it only fires there.
8906               	; 	'
8907               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
8908               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
8909               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
8910               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
8911               	; 	' journey.
8912               	; 	'
8913               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
8914               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
8915               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.
8916               	; 	hfd = fdv

9400 DDE8 0450  20 	b *r0
9401               	;
9402               	; 	' ======================================================================
9403               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9404               	; 	' ======================================================================
9405               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9406               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9407               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9408               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9409               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9410               	; 	'
9411               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9412               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9413               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9414               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9415               	; 	' broken.
9416               	; 	'
9417               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9418               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9419               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9420               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9421               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9422               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9423               	; 	'
9424               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9425               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9426               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9427               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9428               	; coll_obst:
9429               cvb_COLL_OBST
9430               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
9431 DDEA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DDEC 219A     
9432 DDEE 0201  20 	li r1,768   ; 3*256
     DDF0 0300     
9433 DDF2 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
9434 DDF4 1302  14 	jeq cv684

     DDF8 DDFE

     E068 2098     
9727               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
9728               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
9729               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
9730               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
9731               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
9732               	; 	' away from.
9733               	; 	'
9734               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
9735               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
9736               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
9737               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.
9738               	; 	'
9739               	; 	' RADIOS ARE EXEMPT, for the reason they are exempt from the crook's-floor
9740               	; 	' rule: they are CHARACTERS stamped into the name table, not sprites, so
9741               	; 	' zeroing the kind would stop them colliding while leaving them plainly
9742               	; 	' visible on the shelf. A fixture that is still there has to still be
9743               	; 	' there. Only the sprite hazards -- balls, carts, biplanes -- come off.
9744               	; 	chb = klv + klv

10020               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
10021               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
10022               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
10023               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
10024               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
10025               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
10026               	; 	'
10027               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
10028               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
10029               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
10030               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.
10031               	; 	IF klx > hx THEN hdd = klx - hx ELSE hdd = hx - klx

10093               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10094               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10095               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10096               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10097               	; 	'
10098               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10099               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10100               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10101               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10102               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10103               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10104               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

10249               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10250               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10251               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10252               	; 		'
10253               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10254               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10255               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10256               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two

10539               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10540               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10541               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10542               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10543               	; 	'
10544               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10545               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10546               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10547               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10548               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10549               	; 	'
10550               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10551               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10552               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10553               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10554               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10555               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10556               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10557               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10633               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
10634               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
10635               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
10636               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
10637               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
10638               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
10639               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10640               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10641               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10642               	; 	'
10643               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10644               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10645               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10646               	; 	' a jump.
10647               	; 	'
10648               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10649               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
10650               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
10651               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
10652               	; 	'
10653               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10654               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10655               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
10656               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
10657               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
10658               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
10659               	; 	'
10660               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
10661               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
10662               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
10663               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
10664               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
10665               	; 	'
10666               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
10667               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
10668               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
10669               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
10670               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.

11138 EB82 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11139 EB84 0450  20 	b *r0
11140               	;
11141               	; 	' ======================================================================
11142               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11143               	; 	' ======================================================================
11144               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11145               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11146               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11147               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11148               	; 	'
11149               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11150               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11151               	; 	'
11152               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11153               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11154               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11155               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11156               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11157               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11158               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11159               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11160               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11161               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11162               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11163               	; esc_tick:
11164               cvb_ESC_TICK
11165               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11166               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11167               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11168               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11169               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11170 EB86 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB88 2188     
11171 EB8A 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EB8C 0100     
11172 EB8E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11173 EB90 1402  14 	jhe cv824
11174 EB92 0460  28 	b @cv823
     EB94 EBAA     
11175               cv824
11176               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
11177 EB96 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EB98 2188     
11178 EB9A 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     EB9C 0700     
11179 EB9E 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11180 EBA0 1A02  14 	jl cv826
11181 EBA2 0460  28 	b @cv825
     EBA4 EBAA     
11182               cv826
11183 EBA6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11184 EBA8 0450  20 	b *r0
11185               cv825
11186               	; 	END IF
11187               cv823
11188               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11189               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11190               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11191               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11192               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11193               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11194               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11195               	; 	'
11196               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11197               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11198               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11199               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11200               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11201               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11202               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11203               	; 	escp = escp + 1
11204 EBAA D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EBAC 20B0     
11205 EBAE 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EBB0 0100     
11206 EBB2 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EBB4 20B0     
11207               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
11208 EBB6 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     EBB8 0400     
11209 EBBA 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11210 EBBC 1402  14 	jhe cv828
11211 EBBE 0460  28 	b @cv827
     EBC0 EBC8     
11212               cv828
11213 EBC2 04C0  14 	clr r0
11214 EBC4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EBC6 20B0     
11215               cv827
11216               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11217               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11218               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11219               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11220               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11221               	; 	'
11222               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11223               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11224               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11225               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11226               	; 	'
11227               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11228               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11229               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11230               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11231               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
11232               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
11233               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
11234               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
11235               	; 	'
11236               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
11237               	; 	' move at all.

11499               	;
11500               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11501               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11502               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11503               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11504               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11505               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11506               	; 	'
11507               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11508               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11509               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11510               	; 	'
11511               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11512               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11513               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11514               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11515               	; 	'
11516               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11517               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11518               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11519               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11520               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11521               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11522               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11523               	; 	'
11524               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11525               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11526               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11527               	; 	'
11528               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11529               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11530               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11531               	; scan_furn:
11532               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11805               	; 	' THE FLIGHT IS BLACK, and it has to be written per character because the
11806               	; 	' colour table only knows rows -- the same reason the two actors carry
11807               	; 	' their own colour (scan_mark).
11808               	; 	'
11809               	; 	' This runs BEFORE the markers every tick, which is the whole ordering
11810               	; 	' the radar needs: the lift car is painted first and the flights next, so
11811               	; 	' a Kop or a crook standing on either of them paints over it. Nothing
11812               	; 	' moves aside to make room -- the pixels are simply ORed together and the
11813               	; 	' last colour written wins, so an actor in the lift shaft shows as an
11814               	; 	' actor rather than shifting anywhere.

11948 F15A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11949 F15C 0450  20 	b *r0
11950               	;
11951               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
11952               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
11953               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11954               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11955               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11956               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11957               	; scan_wipe:
11958               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
11959               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
11960               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
11961               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
11962               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
11963               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
11964               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
11965               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
11966               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
11967               	; 	'
11968               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
11969               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
11970               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
11971               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
11972               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
11973               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
11974               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
11975               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
11976               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
11977               	; 	'
11978               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
11979               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
11980               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
11981               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
11982               	; 	'
11983               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
11984               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
11985               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
11986 F15E 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     F160 1680

12056               	;
12057               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12058               	; 	'
12059               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12060               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12061               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12062               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12063               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12064               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12065               	; 	'
12066               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12067               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12068               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12069               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12070               	; 	'
12071               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12072               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12073               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12074               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12075               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12076               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12077               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12078               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12079               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12080               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12081               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.

12627               	; 	' falls through to the flash -- no GOTO, so the whole routine stays on
12628               	; 	' one traceable path

12632               	; 	' frame is busiest, which is when the player needs it most.

12794               	;
12795               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12796               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12797               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12798               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12799               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12800               	; 	'
12801               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12802               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12803               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12804               	; 	'
12805               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12806               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12807               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12808               	; 	'
12809               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12810               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).

12913 F8AC 0460  28 	b @cvb_BN_LOOP
     F8AE F800     
12914               	;
12915               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12916               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12917               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12918               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12919               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12920               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12921               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12922               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12923               	; 	'
12924               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12925               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12926               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12927               	;
12928               	; do_escape:
12929               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
12930               	; 	escapd = 0

     F8B4 2102

12940               	; 	' flags are cleared -- clearing first made the biplane test dead code and

     F8EE A980     
12967 F8F0 EB48     	data cvb_HIDE_ALL
12968               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
12969               	; 	'
12970               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
12971               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
12972               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
12973               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
12974               	; 	'
12975               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
12976               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
12977               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
12978               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on
12979               	; 	' the playfield is that colour.
12980               	; 	'
12981               	; 	' THIRTEEN WIDE, WHICH IS THE LONGEST MESSAGE PLUS ONE EITHER SIDE.
12982               	; 	' HE GOT AWAY and THE BIPLANE are both eleven. Every string below is padded
12983               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it
12984               	; 	' at columns 10-22 -- centre 16, the screen's.
12985               	; 	'
12986               	; 	' THE REASON IS ALWAYS ON THE SAME ROW, and that is what lets one layout
12987               	; 	' serve both cases. Losing a life draws three rows around it; losing the
12988               	; 	' last one draws two more ON TOP, so GAME OVER appears above the reason
12989               	; 	' rather than replacing it. Printing the reason at a different row for each
12990               	; 	' case would need `PRINT AT` with a variable, and every other PRINT in this
12991               	; 	' program uses a constant.
12992               	; 	PRINT AT 330,"             "
12993 F8F2 0200  20 	li r0,330
     F8F4 014A

     F8F8 8342

     F8FC F90C

13134               	; 		' globals, so a starting Krook or Kop count typed on the setup
13135               	; 		' screen otherwise applied to every game after it -- including one
13136               	; 		' started by somebody who never saw the screen and had no way to

13155               	;
13156               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13157               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13158               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13159               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13160               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13161               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13162               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13163               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13164               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13165               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13166               	; 	' Harry was caught.
13167               	; snd_off:
13168               cvb_SND_OFF

13620 FE5E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
13621 FE60 0450  20 	b *r0
13622               	;
13623               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
13624               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
13625               	; 	'
13626               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
13627               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
13628               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
13629               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
13630               	; 	'
13631               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
13632               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
13633               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
13634               	; 	'
13635               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
13636               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
13637               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
13638               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
13639               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
13640               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
13641               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
13642               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
13643               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
13644               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
13645               	; 	#if TI994A
13646               	; 	BANK 1
13647      019C     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

13658               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a

13660               	; 	'

13785               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
13786               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
13787               	; 	'
13788               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
13789               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
13790               	; 	' being small sprites.
13791               	; 	'
13792               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
13793               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
13794               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
13795               	; 	'
13796               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
13797               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
13798               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13799               	;
13800               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
13801               cvb_SPR_KELLY

14798               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
14799               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
14800               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
14801               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
14802               	;
14803               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
14804               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
14805               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
14806               	; 	' unreadable.
14807               	;
14808               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

14917               	; 	'   178 ENDWALL
14918               	; 	'   179 SCANBK
14919               	; 	'   180 SLABP
14920               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
14921               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
14922               	; 	'
14923               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
14924               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
14925               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
14926               	; 	'
14927               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
14928               	; 	' blit is
14929               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
14930               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
14931               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
14932               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
14933               	; 	'
14934               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
14935               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
14936               	; 	'
14937               	; 	' template ids:
14938               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
14939               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
14940               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
14941               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
14942               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
14943               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
14944               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
14945               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
14946               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
14947               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
14948               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
14949               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
14950               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
14951               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
14952               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
14953               	;
14954               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
14955               cvb_STOR_TPL
14956               	; 	DATA BYTE 154,154,154,154,107,154,154,154,154,154,154,107,154,154,154,154
